**Hong Kong**[^cite_note-20] is a [special administrative region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_administrative_regions_of_China) of [China](5405.md). Situated on China's southern coast just south of [Shenzhen](83368.md), it consists of [Hong Kong Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Island), [Kowloon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon), and the [New Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Territories). With 7.5 million residents in a 1,114-square-kilometre (430 sq mi) territory, Hong Kong is the [fourth-most densely populated region](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density) in the world.

Hong Kong was established as a [colony of the British Empire](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Hong_Kong) after the [Qing dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qing_dynasty) ceded [Hong Kong Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Island) in 1841–1842 as a result of losing the [First Opium War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Opium_War). The colony expanded to the [Kowloon Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon_Peninsula) in 1860 and was further extended when the United Kingdom obtained a [99-year lease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/99-year_lease) of the New Territories in 1898. Hong Kong was [occupied](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_Hong_Kong) by [Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Empire_of_Japan) from [1941](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Hong_Kong) to [1945](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liberation_Day_\(Hong_Kong\)) during [World War II](32927.md). The territory was [handed over from the United Kingdom to China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handover_of_Hong_Kong) in 1997. Hong Kong maintains separate governing and economic systems from those of [mainland China](55079.md) under the principle of [one country, two systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_country,_two_systems).[^cite_note-21]

Originally an area of farming and fishing villages,[^cite_note-CarrollEarlyHistory-22][^cite_note-FOOTNOTERen2010221-23] Hong Kong is now one of the world's most significant [financial centres](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre). Hong Kong is the world's [third-ranked global financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index) behind [New York City](645042.md) and [London](17867.md), the ninth-largest exporter, and the eighth-largest importer in the world. Its currency, the [Hong Kong dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_dollar), is the [ninth-most traded currency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Template\:Most_traded_currencies) globally. Home to the [third-highest number of billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_number_of_billionaires) of any city in the world, Hong Kong has the second largest number of [ultra high-net-worth individuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultra_high-net-worth_individual).[^cite_note-24][^cite_note-25][^cite_note-26][^cite_note-27] The city has one of the highest [per capita incomes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(PPP\)_per_capita), although severe [income inequality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_income_equality) still exists among the population.

Hong Kong has the [most skyscrapers of any city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_with_the_most_skyscrapers) in the world. [Housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_in_Hong_Kong) is consistently in high demand, making the city of Hong Kong the most expensive residential property market in the world and one of the [most expensive cities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_cities_for_expatriate_employees) in the world.[^cite_note-28][^cite_note-29][^cite_note-30] It is also one of [the most visited cities in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_international_visitors).

Hong Kong is a [highly developed territory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_country) and has a [Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_Development_Index) (HDI) of 0.955, [ranking eighth in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index), and is currently the only place in Asia to be in the top ten. Hong Kong is categorized as an [Alpha+ city](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_city) by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network). The city has the [highest life expectancy in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_life_expectancy), and has a [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Hong_Kong) usage exceeding 90 percent.

|  |  |

The name of the territory, first [romanised](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanization) as "He-Ong-Kong" in 1780,[^cite_note-33] originally referred to a small inlet located between [Aberdeen Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ap_Lei_Chau) and the southern coast of Hong Kong Island. [Aberdeen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aberdeen,_Hong_Kong) was an initial point of contact between British sailors and local fishermen.[^cite_note-34] Although the source of the romanised name is unknown, it is generally believed to be an early phonetic rendering of the [Cantonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese) (or [Tanka Cantonese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tanka_people#Dialect)) phrase *hēung góng*. The name translates as "fragrant harbour" or "incense harbour".[^cite_note-HerbariumName-31][^cite_note-CarrollHKName-32][^cite_note-etym-35] "Fragrant" may refer to the sweet taste of the harbour's freshwater influx from the Pearl River or to the odour from incense factories lining the coast of northern [Kowloon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon). The incense was stored near Aberdeen Harbour for export before [Victoria Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Harbour) was developed.[^cite_note-etym-35] [Sir John Davis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Francis_Davis) (the second colonial governor) offered an alternative origin; Davis said that the name derived from "Hoong-keang" ("red torrent"), reflecting the colour of soil over which a waterfall on the island flowed.[^cite_note-36]

The simplified name *Hong Kong* was frequently used by 1810.[^cite_note-37] The name was also commonly written as the single word *Hongkong* until 1926, when the government officially adopted the two-word name.[^cite_note-38] Some corporations founded during the early colonial era still keep this name, including [Hongkong Land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongkong_Land), [Hongkong Electric Company](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongkong_Electric_Company), [Hongkong and Shanghai Hotels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hongkong_and_Shanghai_Hotels), and [the Hongkong and Shanghai Banking Corporation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Hongkong_and_Shanghai_Banking_Corporation) (HSBC).[^cite_note-39][^cite_note-HSBC-40]

## History

Main article: [History of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Hong_Kong)

For a chronological guide, see [Timeline of Hong Kong history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Hong_Kong_history).

### Prehistory and Imperial China

The earliest known human traces in what is now called Hong Kong are dated by some to 35,000-39,000 years ago during the [Paleolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Paleolithic) period. The claim is based on an archaeological investigation in [Wong Tei Tung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wong_Tei_Tung) in the [Sai Kung Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sai_Kung_Peninsula) in 2003. The archaeological works revealed [knapped](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Knapping) [stone tools](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stone_tool) from deposits dated by optical [luminescence dating](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luminescence_dating).[^cite_note-41]

During the Middle [Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neolithic) period, about 6,000 years ago, the region had been widely occupied by humans.[^cite_note-Meacham2-42] Neolithic to [Bronze Age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bronze_Age) Hong Kong settlers were semi-coastal people. Early inhabitants are believed to have been [Austronesians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Austronesian_peoples) in the Middle Neolithic period, and later the [Yue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baiyue) people.[^cite_note-Meacham2-42] As hinted by the archaeological works in Sha Ha, Sai Kung, rice cultivation had been introduced since the [Late Neolithic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Late_Neolithic) period.[^cite_note-Li38-43] Bronze Age Hong Kong featured coarse pottery, hard pottery, quartz and stone jewelry, as well as small bronze implements.[^cite_note-Meacham2-42]



[Sung Wong Toi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sung_Wong_Toi)

The [Qin dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_dynasty) incorporated the Hong Kong area into China for the first time in 214 BCE, after [conquering the indigenous Baiyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qin_campaign_against_the_Yue_tribes).[^cite_note-44] The region was consolidated under the [Nanyue](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nanyue) kingdom (a predecessor state of [Vietnam](202354.md)) after the Qin collapse,[^cite_note-KeatGinOoi-45] and it was recaptured by China after the [Han conquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Han_conquest_of_Nanyue).[^cite_note-46] During the [Mongol conquest of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mongol_conquest_of_the_Song_dynasty) in the 13th century, the [southern Song](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Song_dynasty) court was briefly located in modern-day [Kowloon City](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon_City) (the [Sung Wong Toi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sung_Wong_Toi) site) before its final defeat in the 1279 [Battle of Yamen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Yamen) by the [Yuan dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Yuan_dynasty).[^cite_note-Barber-47] By the end of the Yuan dynasty, seven large families had settled in the region and owned most of the land. Settlers from nearby provinces migrated to Kowloon throughout the [Ming dynasty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ming_dynasty).[^cite_note-48]

The earliest known European visitor was [Portuguese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kingdom_of_Portugal) explorer [Jorge Álvares](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jorge_%C3%81lvares), who arrived in 1513.[^cite_note-49][^cite_note-50] Portuguese merchants established a trading post called [Tamão](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tam%C3%A3o) in Hong Kong waters and began regular trade with southern China. Although the traders were expelled after [military clashes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Tunmen) in the 1520s,[^cite_note-51] Portuguese-Chinese trade relations were [re-established by 1549](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Luso-Chinese_agreement). Portugal acquired a [permanent lease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-Portuguese_Treaty_of_Peking) for [Macau](19068.md) in 1887.[^cite_note-52]

After the [Qing conquest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transition_from_Ming_to_Qing), maritime trade was banned under the *[Haijin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Haijin)* policies. From 1661 to 1683, the population of most of the area that forms present-day Hong Kong was cleared under the [Great Clearance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Clearance), turning the region into a wasteland.[^cite_note-HK_story-53] The [Kangxi Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kangxi_Emperor) lifted the maritime trade prohibition, allowing foreigners to enter Chinese ports in 1684.[^cite_note-54] Qing authorities established the [Canton System](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton_System) in 1757 to regulate trade more strictly, restricting non-Russian ships to the port of [Canton](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou).[^cite_note-55] Although European demand for Chinese commodities like tea, silk, and porcelain was high, Chinese interest in European manufactured goods was insignificant, so that Chinese goods could only be bought with precious metals. To reduce the trade imbalance, the British sold large amounts of Indian [opium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opium) to China. Faced with a drug crisis, Qing officials pursued ever more aggressive actions to halt the opium trade.[^cite_note-56]

### British colony

Main article: [British Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Hong_Kong)



Hong Kong in 1868, photograph by [John Thomson](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Thomson_\(photographer\))

In 1839, the [Daoguang Emperor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Daoguang_Emperor) rejected proposals to legalise and tax [opium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opium) and ordered imperial commissioner [Lin Zexu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lin_Zexu) to eradicate the opium trade. The commissioner destroyed opium stockpiles and halted all foreign trade,[^cite_note-57] triggering a British military response and the [First Opium War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/First_Opium_War). The Qing surrendered early in the war and ceded Hong Kong Island in the [Convention of Chuenpi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_Chuenpi), signed by [Charles Elliot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Charles_Elliot) and [Qishan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Qishan_\(official\)). British forces began controlling Hong Kong shortly after the signing of the convention, on 26 January 1841.[^cite_note-58] However, both countries were dissatisfied and did not ratify the agreement.[^cite_note-59] After more than a year of further hostilities, Hong Kong Island was formally ceded to the [United Kingdom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/United_Kingdom_of_Great_Britain_and_Ireland) in the 1842 [Treaty of Nanking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Treaty_of_Nanking).[^cite_note-Courtauld-60]

Administrative infrastructure was quickly built by early 1842, but piracy, disease, and hostile Qing policies initially prevented the government from attracting commerce. Conditions on the island improved during the [Taiping Rebellion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taiping_Rebellion) in the 1850s, when many Chinese refugees, including wealthy merchants, fled mainland turbulence and settled in the colony.[^cite_note-CarrollEarlyHistory-22] Further tensions between the British and Qing over the opium trade escalated into the [Second Opium War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Opium_War). The Qing were again defeated and were forced to give up [Kowloon Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon_Peninsula) and [Stonecutters Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stonecutters_Island) in the [Convention of Peking](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Convention_of_Peking).[^cite_note-SecondOpiumWar-61] By the end of this war, Hong Kong had evolved from a transient colonial outpost into a major [entrepôt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Entrep%C3%B4t). Rapid economic improvement during the 1850s attracted foreign investment, as potential stakeholders became more confident in Hong Kong's future.[^cite_note-62]

The colony was further expanded in 1898 when the United Kingdom obtained a 99-year lease of the New Territories.[^cite_note-NTLease-63] The [University of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Hong_Kong) was established in 1911 as the territory's first institution of higher education.[^cite_note-64] [Kai Tak Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kai_Tak_Airport) began operation in 1924, and the colony avoided a prolonged economic downturn after the 1925–26 [Canton–Hong Kong strike](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canton%E2%80%93Hong_Kong_strike).[^cite_note-KaiTak-65][^cite_note-66] At the start of the [Second Sino-Japanese War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Sino-Japanese_War) in 1937, Governor [Geoffry Northcote](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geoffry_Northcote) declared Hong Kong a neutral zone to safeguard its status as a free port.[^cite_note-67] The colonial government prepared for a possible attack, evacuating all British women and children in 1940.[^cite_note-68] The [Imperial Japanese Army](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Imperial_Japanese_Army) [attacked Hong Kong on 8 December 1941](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Battle_of_Hong_Kong), the same morning as its [attack on Pearl Harbor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attack_on_Pearl_Harbor).[^cite_note-69] Hong Kong was [occupied by Japan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Japanese_occupation_of_Hong_Kong) for almost four years before the British resumed control on 30 August 1945.[^cite_note-70]



The [flag of British Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flag_of_Hong_Kong_\(1871%E2%80%931997\)) from 1959 to 1997



[Peking Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peking_Road) in Tsim Sha Tsui in 1971

Its population rebounded quickly after the war, as skilled Chinese migrants fled from the [Chinese Civil War](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Civil_War) and more refugees crossed the border when the [Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) took control of mainland China in 1949.[^cite_note-Wiltshire-71] Hong Kong became the first of the [Four Asian Tiger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Asian_Tigers) economies to industrialise during the 1950s.[^cite_note-72] With a rapidly increasing population, the colonial government attempted reforms to improve infrastructure and public services. The [public-housing estate programme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_housing_in_Hong_Kong), [Independent Commission Against Corruption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independent_Commission_Against_Corruption_\(Hong_Kong\)), and [Mass Transit Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTR) were all established during the post-war decades to provide safer housing, integrity in the civil service, and more reliable transportation.[^cite_note-73][^cite_note-74]

Nevertheless, widespread public discontent led to multiple protests from the 1950s to 1980s, including pro-[Republic of China](25734.md) and pro-[Chinese Communist Party](7175.md) demonstrations. In the [1967 Hong Kong riots](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1967_Hong_Kong_riots), pro-[PRC](5405.md) protestors clashed with the British colonial government. As many as 51 were killed, and 802 were injured in the violence, including dozens killed by the [Royal Hong Kong Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Royal_Hong_Kong_Police) via beatings and shootings.[^cite_note-75]

Although the territory's manufacturing competitiveness gradually declined due to rising labour and property costs, it transitioned to a service-based economy. By the early 1990s, Hong Kong had established itself as a global [financial centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Financial_centre) and shipping hub.[^cite_note-76]

### Chinese special administrative region

Further information: [Handover of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Handover_of_Hong_Kong)



[Victoria Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Harbour) and [Hong Kong Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Island), 2007

The colony faced an uncertain future as the end of the New Territories lease approached, and [Governor Murray MacLehose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Murray_MacLehose,_Baron_MacLehose_of_Beoch) raised the question of Hong Kong's status with [Deng Xiaoping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Deng_Xiaoping) in 1979.[^cite_note-77] Diplomatic negotiations with China resulted in the 1984 [Sino-British Joint Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-British_Joint_Declaration), in which the United Kingdom agreed to the handover of the colony in 1997, and China would guarantee Hong Kong's economic and political systems for 50 years after the handover.[^cite_note-Carroll181-78] The impending handover triggered a [wave of mass emigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waves_of_mass_migrations_from_Hong_Kong) as residents feared an erosion of civil rights, the rule of law, and quality of life.[^cite_note-79] Over half a million people left the territory during the peak migration period, from 1987 to 1996.[^cite_note-2002Pop-80] The Legislative Council became a [fully elected legislature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1995_Hong_Kong_legislative_election) for the first time in 1995. It extensively expanded its functions and organisations throughout the last years of the colonial rule.[^cite_note-81] The handover of Hong Kong to China was at midnight on 1 July 1997, after 156 years of British rule.[^cite_note-NYTHandover-82]

Immediately after the handover, Hong Kong was severely affected by several crises. The Hong Kong government was forced to use substantial [foreign exchange reserves](1015869.md) to maintain the Hong Kong dollar's currency peg during the [1997 Asian financial crisis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1997_Asian_financial_crisis),[^cite_note-Wiltshire-71] and the recovery from this was muted by an [H5N1 avian-flu](1019908.md) outbreak[^cite_note-83] and a housing surplus.[^cite_note-84] This was followed by the [2003 SARS epidemic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2002%E2%80%932004_SARS_outbreak), during which the territory experienced its most serious economic downturn.[^cite_note-85]

Chinese communists portrayed the return of Hong Kong as a key moment in the PRC's rise to [great power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_power) status.[^cite_note-\:172-86]<sup>: 51 </sup>



[Hong Kong protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests), August 2019

Political debates after the handover have centred around the region's [democratic development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_development_in_Hong_Kong) and the [Chinese central government](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Council_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China)'s adherence to the "one country, two systems" principle. After reversal of the last colonial era Legislative Council [democratic reforms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1994_Hong_Kong_electoral_reform) following the handover,[^cite_note-87] the regional government unsuccessfully attempted to enact [national security legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Security_\(Legislative_Provisions\)_Bill_2003) pursuant to [Article 23 of the Basic Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Basic_Law_Article_23).[^cite_note-88] The central government decision to implement [nominee pre-screening](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_NPCSC_Decision_on_Hong_Kong) before allowing [chief executive elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014%E2%80%932015_Hong_Kong_electoral_reform) triggered a series of [protests in 2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Hong_Kong_protests) which became known as the Umbrella Revolution.[^cite_note-89] Discrepancies in the electoral registry and disqualification of elected legislators after the [2016 Legislative Council elections](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Hong_Kong_legislative_election)[^cite_note-90][^cite_note-91][^cite_note-92] and enforcement of national law in the [West Kowloon high-speed railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_West_Kowloon_railway_station) raised further concerns about the region's autonomy.[^cite_note-93] In June 2019, [mass protests erupted](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests) in response to a [proposed extradition amendment bill](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019_Hong_Kong_extradition_bill) permitting the extradition of fugitives to mainland China. The protests are the largest in Hong Kong's history,[^cite_note-EconomistRestoreCalm-94] with organisers claiming to have attracted more than three million Hong Kong residents.

#### Following the 2019 protests



A tram with slogans promoting the National Security Law printed on it

The Hong Kong regional government and the Chinese central government responded to the protests with many administrative measures to quell dissent. In June 2020, the Legislative Council passed the [National Anthem Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Anthem_Ordinance), which criminalised "insults to the national anthem of China".[^cite_note-95] The Chinese central government meanwhile enacted the [Hong Kong national security law](64033197.md) to help quell protests in the region.[^cite_note-cnn20200630-96] Nine months later, in March 2021, the Chinese central government introduced [amendments to Hong Kong's electoral system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hong_Kong_electoral_changes), which included the reduction of directly elected seats in the Legislative Council and the requirement that all candidates be vetted and approved by a Beijing-appointed [Candidate Eligibility Review Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candidate_Eligibility_Review_Committee).[^cite_note-france2420210311-97]

In July 2023, [Hong Kong's Legislative Council](273946.md) passed reforms slashing directly elected [District Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Council_Eligibility_Review_Committee) seats and establishing a vetting committee for candidates.[^cite_note-dc2023_freepress01-98][^cite_note-dc2023_gld01-99][^cite_note-dc2023_gld02-100]

In terms of international connectedness, as of 2024, the city was one of eight worldwide that was classified as an "Alpha+" city by the [Globalization and World Cities Research Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network).[^cite_note-101]

## Government and politics

Main articles: [Government of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Hong_Kong), [Politics of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Hong_Kong), [Elections in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elections_in_Hong_Kong), and [Separation of powers in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Separation_of_powers_in_Hong_Kong)

Large, round room with desks and a dais

Since 2012, the legislature has met in the [Tamar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamar,_Hong_Kong) [Legislative Council Complex](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislative_Council_Complex).

Hong Kong is a [special administrative region of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_administrative_regions_of_China), with executive, legislative, and judicial powers [devolved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Devolution) from the [national government](23239.md).[^cite_note-102] The Sino-British Joint Declaration provided for economic and administrative continuity through the handover,[^cite_note-Carroll181-78] resulting in an [executive-led](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_\(government\)) governing system largely inherited from the territory's history as a British colony.[^cite_note-BasicLaw15-103] Under these terms and the "one country, two systems" principle, the [Basic Law of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Basic_Law) is the regional constitution.[^cite_note-104] The regional government is composed of three branches:

- *[Executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Government_of_Hong_Kong):* As Hong Kong operates under an executive-led system, the [Chief Executive](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Executive_of_Hong_Kong) is responsible for enforcing regional law,

  [^cite_note-BasicLaw15-103]

  &#x20;can force reconsideration of legislation,

  [^cite_note-105]

  &#x20;and appoints [Executive Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Executive_Council_of_Hong_Kong) members and [principal officials](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Principal_officials_of_Hong_Kong).

  [^cite_note-106]

  &#x20;Acting with the Executive Council, the [Chief Executive-in-Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King-in-Council#The_Commonwealth) can propose new bills, issue [subordinate legislation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Primary_and_secondary_legislation), and has authority to [dissolve](652293.md) the legislature.

  [^cite_note-107]

  &#x20;In [states of emergency](298608.md) or public danger, the [Chief Executive-in-Council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chief_Executive-in-Council) is further [empowered](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_Regulations_Ordinance) to enact any regulation necessary to restore public order.

  [^cite_note-108]

- *[Legislature](273946.md):* The unicameral [Legislative Council](273946.md) enacts regional law, approves budgets, and has the power to [impeach](15328.md) a sitting chief executive.

  [^cite_note-109]

- *[Judiciary](1501145.md):* The [Hong Kong Court of Final Appeal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Court_of_Final_Appeal) and lower courts interpret laws and overturn those inconsistent with the Basic Law.

  [^cite_note-Gittings150-110]

  &#x20;The chief executive appoints judges on the advice of a recommendation commission.

  [^cite_note-111]

The chief executive is the [head of government](37570.md), serving for a maximum of two five-year terms; however, as of 2026, no chief executive has completed the maximum two five-year term limit.[^cite_note-112] The [State Council](44498.md) (led by the [Premier of China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Premier_of_China)) appoints the chief executive after nomination by the [Election Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_Committee), which is composed of 1500 business, community, and government leaders.[^cite_note-113][^cite_note-114][^cite_note-115]



Hong Kong Government House

The Legislative Council has 90 members, each serving a four-year term. Twenty are directly elected from [geographical constituencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geographical_constituency), thirty represent [functional constituencies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functional_constituency_\(Hong_Kong\)) (FC), and forty are local chosen by an [election committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Election_Committee_\(constituency\)) consisting of representatives appointed by the Chinese central government.[^cite_note-overhaul_HK-116] Thirty FC councillors are selected from limited electorates representing sectors of the economy or special interest groups.[^cite_note-LegCoVoting-117] Geographical constituency elected members are chosen by single non-transferable vote ([SNTV](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single_non-transferable_vote)), yielding two candidates per GC. The 30 limited electorate functional constituencies fill their seats using [first-past-the-post](261709.md) or [instant-runoff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Instant-runoff_voting) voting.[^cite_note-LegCoVoting-117]

Twenty-two political parties had representatives elected to the Legislative Council in the [2016 election](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2016_Hong_Kong_legislative_election).[^cite_note-118] These parties have aligned themselves into three ideological groups: the [pro-Beijing camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Beijing_camp_\(Hong_Kong\)) (the current government), the [pro-democracy camp](3291227.md), and [localist groups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Localism_in_Hong_Kong).[^cite_note-119] However, by [2021](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hong_Kong_legislative_election), the pro-democracy camp and the localist groups lost all representation in the Legislative Council as a result of the [2021 electoral changes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hong_Kong_electoral_changes) imposed by the [National People's Congress](38878740.md),[^cite_note-120] and since [2025](78934480.md) all 90 members of the Legislative Council have been from the pro-Beijing camp. The Chinese Communist Party does not have an official political presence in Hong Kong, and it does not contest local elections.[^cite_note-121] Hong Kong is represented in the National People's Congress by 36 deputies chosen through an electoral college and 203 delegates in the [National Committee of the Chinese People's Political Consultative Conference](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Committee_of_the_Chinese_People's_Political_Consultative_Conference) appointed by the central government.[^cite_note-NationalReps-10]

The grey dome and front gable of a granite neo-classical building, with a skyscraper in the background against a clear blue sky

The [Court of Final Appeal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Final_Appeal_Building) formerly housed the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_\(Hong_Kong\)) and the [Legislative Council](273946.md).

There are major differences between Hong Kong's legal system and the rest of China's.[^cite_note-Gittings150-110] Its judicial system is based on [common law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Common_law), continuing the legal tradition established during British rule.[^cite_note-122] Local courts may refer to precedents set in [English law](85441.md) and overseas jurisprudence.[^cite_note-123] However, [mainland criminal procedure law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Criminal_Procedure_Law_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) applies to cases investigated by the [Office for Safeguarding National Security of the CPG in the HKSAR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Office_for_Safeguarding_National_Security).[^cite_note-NPC_Observer-124] Interpretative and amending power over the Basic Law and jurisdiction over acts of state lie with the central authority, making regional courts ultimately subordinate to the mainland's [socialist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Socialist_law) [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)) system.[^cite_note-125] Decisions made by the [Standing Committee of the National People's Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Committee_of_the_National_People's_Congress) override any territorial judicial process.[^cite_note-HCAL185/2016-126] Furthermore, in circumstances where the Standing Committee declares a state of emergency in Hong Kong, the State Council may enforce national law in the region.[^cite_note-BasicLawA18-127]

The territory's jurisdictional independence is most apparent in its [immigration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visa_policy_of_Hong_Kong) and [taxation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tax) policies. The [Immigration Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Immigration_Department_\(Hong_Kong\)) issues [passports for Chinese nationals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Special_Administrative_Region_passport) which differ from those of the mainland or Macau,[^cite_note-128] and the region maintains a [regulated border](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Boundaries_of_Hong_Kong) with the rest of the country. All travellers between Hong Kong, mainland China, and Macau must pass through border controls, regardless of nationality.[^cite_note-129] Mainland Chinese residents do not have [right of abode in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Right_of_abode_in_Hong_Kong) and are subject to immigration controls.[^cite_note-130] Public finances are handled separately from the national government, and taxes levied in Hong Kong do not fund the central authority.[^cite_note-131][^cite_note-132]

The [Hong Kong Garrison](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Garrison) of the [People's Liberation Army](66890.md) is responsible for the region's defence.[^cite_note-133] Although the [Chairman of the Central Military Commission](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chairman_of_the_Central_Military_Commission_\(China\)) is [supreme commander](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Military_Command_of_the_People's_Republic_of_China) of the armed forces,[^cite_note-134] the regional government may request assistance from the garrison.[^cite_note-135] Hong Kong residents are not required to perform military service, and current law has no provision for local enlistment. The garrison is composed entirely of non-Hongkongers.[^cite_note-136]

The central government and [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](7453497.md) handle diplomatic matters, but Hong Kong retains the ability to maintain separate economic and cultural [relations with foreign nations](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Hong_Kong).[^cite_note-137] The territory actively participates in the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization), the [Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Asia-Pacific_Economic_Cooperation) forum, the [International Olympic Committee](15147.md), and many [United Nations](31769.md) agencies.[^cite_note-138][^cite_note-139][^cite_note-140] It holds membership in other international bodies including the [World Health Organization](33583.md).[^cite_note-\:022-141]<sup>: 201 </sup> The regional government maintains [trade offices](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Economic_and_Trade_Office) in [Greater China](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Greater_China) and other nations.[^cite_note-142]

The imposition of the [Hong Kong national security law](64033197.md) by the [central government in Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_People's_Congress_decision_on_Hong_Kong_national_security_legislation) in June 2020 resulted in the suspension of bilateral extradition treaties by the United Kingdom, Canada, Australia, New Zealand, Finland, and Ireland.[^cite_note-rgmsus-143] The United States ended its preferential economic and trade treatment of Hong Kong in July 2020 because it was no longer able to distinguish Hong Kong as a separate entity from the People's Republic of China.[^cite_note-rgmsus-143][^cite_note-bbcpref-144] In 2024, the [Safeguarding National Security Ordinance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Safeguarding_National_Security_Ordinance) was passed by the Legislative Council to grant officials the power to prosecute crimes such as [treason](31292.md) and [insurrection](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Insurrection). Critics state that this expansion will give more powers to crack down on opposition to the central government of China and the Hong Kong government as well as strike a lasting blow to the partial autonomy China had promised the city in the [Sino-British Joint Declaration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sino-British_Joint_Declaration).[^cite_note-145]

### Administrative divisions

Main articles: [Areas of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Areas_of_Hong_Kong), [Districts of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Hong_Kong), and [List of towns in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_towns_in_Hong_Kong)

Hong Kong's administrative divisions are divided into three levels: [Areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Areas_of_Hong_Kong) (區域), [districts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Districts_of_Hong_Kong) (地區), and sub-districts (分區).[^cite_note-\:12-146] Hong Kong is administratively divided into three areas: [Hong Kong Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Island), [Kowloon](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon), and the [New Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Territories). They are further divided into 18 districts. The area of Hong Kong Island has four districts, the area of Kowloon has five districts, and the area of the New Territories has nine districts.[^cite_note-\:12-146][^cite_note-\:02-147] Each district is represented by a [district council](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_councils_of_Hong_Kong). The district councils advise the government on local issues such as the provision of public facilities, the maintenance of community programmes, cultural promotion, and environmental policy.[^cite_note-district-148][^cite_note-\:4-149][^cite_note-dc2023_gld01-99]

As of 2024, there are a total of 470 district council seats, 88 of which are directly elected.[^cite_note-150] In May 2023, the government proposed [reforms to the District Council electoral system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2023_Hong_Kong_electoral_changes) which significantly cut the number of directly elected seats from 452 to 88, and total seats from 479 to 470. A requirement that district council candidates be vetted and approved by the [District Council Eligibility Review Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Council_Eligibility_Review_Committee) was also implemented. The Legislative Council approved the reforms in July 2023.[^cite_note-dc2023_freepress01-98][^cite_note-dc2023_gld01-99][^cite_note-dc2023_gld02-100]

The main territory of Hong Kong consists of a peninsula bordered to the north by Guangdong province, an island to the south east of the peninsula, and a smaller island to the south. These areas are surrounded by numerous much smaller islands.

### Political reforms and sociopolitical issues

Main articles: [Democratic development in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_development_in_Hong_Kong) and [Human rights in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Human_rights_in_Hong_Kong)



A yellow vertical protest banner which read "We demand real [universal suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage)" was hung on [Lion Rock](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lion_Rock) during the [2014 Hong Kong protests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_Hong_Kong_protests).

Hong Kong is governed by a [hybrid regime](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hybrid_regime) that is not fully [representative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy) of the population. [Legislative Council](273946.md) members elected by functional constituencies composed of professional and special-interest groups are accountable to narrow corporate electorates rather than the general public. This electoral arrangement has ensured a [pro-Beijing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pro-Beijing_camp_\(Hong_Kong\)) majority in the legislature since the handover. Similarly, the chief executive is selected by establishment politicians and corporate members of the Election Committee rather than directly elected.[^cite_note-hybridregime-151] Despite [universal suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_suffrage) being established as ultimate goals for the election of the chief executive and all members of the Legislative Council in [Articles 45](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Basic_Law_Article_45) and 68 of the basic law,[^cite_note-152] the legislature is only partially directly elected, and the executive continues to be nominated by an unrepresentative body.[^cite_note-hybridregime-151] The government has been repeatedly petitioned to introduce direct elections for these positions, but has not introduced these direct elections as of 2026.[^cite_note-153][^cite_note-154]

Ethnic minorities (except those of European ancestry) have marginal representation in government and often experience discrimination in housing, education, and employment.[^cite_note-155][^cite_note-156] Employment vacancies and public service appointments frequently have language requirements that minority job seekers do not meet, and language education resources remain inadequate for Chinese learners.[^cite_note-157][^cite_note-158] [Foreign domestic helpers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_domestic_helpers_in_Hong_Kong), mostly women from the Philippines and Indonesia, have little protection under regional law.[^cite_note-159] Although they live and work in Hong Kong, these workers are not treated as ordinary residents and do not have the right of abode in the territory.[^cite_note-160]

The Joint Declaration guarantees the [Basic Law of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Basic_Law_of_Hong_Kong) for 50 years after the handover.[^cite_note-Carroll181-78] According to legal scholar [Albert Chen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Albert_Chen_Hung-yee), the Basic Law does not have an expiry date and is, unless amended, of continuing validity.[^cite_note-161] In response to [large-scale protests in 2019 and 2020](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2019%E2%80%932020_Hong_Kong_protests), the [Standing Committee of the National People's Congress](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Standing_Committee_of_the_National_People's_Congress) passed the controversial [Hong Kong national security law](64033197.md).[^cite_note-162] The law criminalises secession, subversion, terrorism and collusion with foreign elements and establishes the Office for Safeguarding National Security of the CPG in the HKSAR, an investigative office under Central People's Government authority immune from HKSAR jurisdiction. Some of the aforementioned acts were previously considered protected speech under Hong Kong law.[^cite_note-NPC_Observer-124][^cite_note-163] The United Kingdom considers the law to be a serious violation of the Joint Declaration.[^cite_note-164] In October 2020, the Hong Kong Police arrested seven pro-[democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democracy) politicians over tussles with pro-Beijing politicians in the Legislative Council in May. They were charged with contempt and with interfering with council members, while none of the pro-Beijing lawmakers were detained.[^cite_note-165] Annual commemorations of the [1989 Tiananmen Square protests and massacre](44534.md) were also cancelled amidst fears of violating the national security law.[^cite_note-166] In March 2021, the Chinese central government [changed Hong Kong's electoral system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2021_Hong_Kong_electoral_changes) and established the [Candidate Eligibility Review Committee](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Candidate_Eligibility_Review_Committee), which would be tasked with screening and evaluating political candidates for their "patriotism", effectively crushing the remainder of the [pro-democracy camp](3291227.md).[^cite_note-france2420210311-97]

In February 2024, [Xia Baolong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Xia_Baolong), the head of the [Hong Kong and Macau Affairs Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_and_Macau_Affairs_Office), said that the "[one country, two systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/One_country,_two_systems)" principle would be kept permanently.[^cite_note-167]

## Geography

Main article: [Geography of Hong Kong](13406.md)

See also: [Land reclamation in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation_in_Hong_Kong) and [Environment of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environment_of_Hong_Kong)

Satellite image showing areas of vegetation and conurbation.

Areas of urban development and vegetation are visible in this satellite image.

Hong Kong is situated on China's southern coast, 60 km (37 mi) east of Macau and on the eastern side of the [Pearl River estuary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pearl_River_Delta). The territory is surrounded by the [South China Sea](74209.md) on all sides except the north, where it neighbors the [Guangdong](65109.md) city of [Shenzhen](83368.md) along the [Sham Chun River](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sham_Chun_River). Its standard area is approximately 1,110 km<sup>2</sup> (430 sq mi),[^cite_note-\:0-168] though this figure reaches approximately 2,750 km<sup>2</sup> (1,060 sq mi)<sup>2</sup>[^cite_note-\:0-168] when the open maritime area under its jurisdiction (rather than of [mainland China](55079.md)) is included. The territory consists of [Hong Kong Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Island), the [Kowloon Peninsula](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kowloon_Peninsula), the [New Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Territories), [Lantau Island](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lantau_Island), and over 200 other islands.

Of the standard area, 1,073 km<sup>2</sup> (414 sq mi) is land and 35 km<sup>2</sup> (14 sq mi) is inland water, such as reservoirs, rivers, and ponds.[^cite_note-cia-169] The territory's highest point is [Tai Mo Shan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tai_Mo_Shan), reaching 957 metres (3,140 ft) above sea level.[^cite_note-170] Urban development is primarily concentrated on the Kowloon Peninsula, Hong Kong Island, and within various [new towns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_towns_of_Hong_Kong) throughout the New Territories.[^cite_note-171] Much of this is built on [reclaimed land](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Land_reclamation_in_Hong_Kong); 70 km<sup>2</sup> (27 sq mi) (representing 6% of the total land or about 25% of the territory's developed space) has been reclaimed from the sea.[^cite_note-reclamation-172]



Twisted columnar jointing near the High Island Reservoir East Dam

Undeveloped terrain is hilly to mountainous, with minimal flat land, and consists mostly of grassland, woodland, shrubland, or farmland.[^cite_note-173][^cite_note-174] About 40% of the remaining land area is [country parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conservation_in_Hong_Kong) and nature reserves.[^cite_note-175] The territory has a diverse ecosystem; over 3,000 species of [vascular plants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vascular_plant) occur in the region (300 of which are native to Hong Kong), and thousands of insect, avian, and marine species.[^cite_note-176][^cite_note-177] [Hong Kong UNESCO Global Geopark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_UNESCO_Global_Geopark) was inaugurated on 3 November 2009. It is a single entity of land area over 150 km<sup>2</sup> across parts of the eastern and northeastern [New Territories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/New_Territories).[^cite_note-178] On 18 September 2011, [UNESCO](21786641.md) listed the geopark as part of its [Global Geoparks Network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Geoparks_Network).

The Hong Kong UNESCO Global Geopark consists of two geological regions:[^cite_note-179]

- the [Sai Kung](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sai_Kung_District) Volcanic Rock Region, with its widely distributed [tuff](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tuff) [volcanic rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic_rocks) displaying [prismatic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prism_\(geometry\)) [columnar jointing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Columnar_jointing), which are of international geological significance

- the Northeast New Territories Sedimentary Rock Region, which comprises [sedimentary rocks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sedimentary_rocks) formed in different geologic periods, showcasing the complete [geological history of Hong Kong.](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Geology_of_Hong_Kong)

### Climate



[Hong Kong Observatory](21292238.md) Headquarters

Hong Kong has a [humid subtropical climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Humid_subtropical_climate) ([Köppen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/K%C3%B6ppen_climate_classification) *Cwa*), characteristic of southern China, closely bordering on a [tropical monsoon climate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_monsoon_climate). Summers are long, hot, and humid, with occasional showers and thunderstorms and warm air from the southwest. The humid climate of Hong Kong intensifies summer heat. [Typhoons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Typhoon) occur most often then, sometimes resulting in floods or landslides. Also rarely occurring are [waterspouts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Waterspout) and [tornadoes](37530.md), which occurred at Hong Kong International Airport on 26 September 2020 and at Victoria Harbour on 28 September 2024.[^cite_note-180][^cite_note-181] Winters are short, mild and usually sunny at the beginning, becoming cloudy towards February. Frequent cold fronts bring strong, cooling winds from the north and occasionally result in chilly weather. Autumn is the sunniest season, whilst spring is generally cloudy.[^cite_note-HKClimate-182] Snowfall has been extremely rare in Hong Kong; the last reported instance was on [Tai Mo Shan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tai_Mo_Shan) in 1975.[^cite_note-183] Hong Kong averages 1,709 hours of sunshine per year.[^cite_note-184] Historic temperature extremes at the [Hong Kong Observatory](21292238.md) are 36.9 °C (98.4 °F) on 9 August 2026 and 0.0 °C (32.0 °F) on 18 January 1893, record highest daily minimum temperature there was 30.2 °C (86.4 °F) on 12 August 2026 and the lowest daily maximum temperature there was 3.2 °C (37.8 °F) on 16 January 1893.[^cite_note-185][^cite_note-186][^cite_note-187] The highest and lowest recorded temperatures in all of Hong Kong are 39.8 °C (104 °F) at [Sheung Shui](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sheung_Shui) on 9 August 2026,[^cite_note-188] and −6.0 °C (21.2 °F) at Tai Mo Shan [on 24 January 2016](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/January_2016_East_Asia_cold_wave).[^cite_note-189]

### Environment

Main article: [Air pollution in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution_in_Hong_Kong)

[Air pollution in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_pollution_in_Hong_Kong) is considered a major problem. It became a concern soon after the start of the 2000s. According to the [Environmental Protection Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Environmental_Protection_Department) (EPD), the major air [pollutants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pollutant) in Hong Kong include [nitrogen oxides](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nitrogen_oxides) (NO<sub>x</sub>), [sulphur dioxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sulphur_dioxide) (SO<sub>2</sub>), respirable suspended [particulates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Particulates) (RSP or PM<sub>10</sub>), fine suspended particulates (FSP or PM<sub>2.5</sub>), [volatile organic compounds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volatile_organic_compound) (VOC), [carbon monoxide](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Carbon_monoxide) (CO),[^cite_note-emissioninventory-194] [ozone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ozone) and [lead](17747.md).[^cite_note-AQOs-195]

### Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Hong_Kong)



[Monster Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Monster_Building), a famous group of residential buildings in [Quarry Bay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quarry_Bay)

Hong Kong has the world's [largest number of skyscrapers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_with_the_most_skyscrapers), with 554 towers taller than 150 metres (490 ft),[^cite_note-SkyscraperCenter-196] and the third-largest number of high-rise buildings in the world.[^cite_note-197] The lack of available space restricted development to high-density residential [tenements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tenement) and commercial complexes packed closely together on buildable land.[^cite_note-198] [Single-family detached homes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Single-family_detached_home) are uncommon and generally only found in outlying areas.[^cite_note-199] The [International Commerce Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Commerce_Centre) and [Two International Finance Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Finance_Centre_\(Hong_Kong\)) are the [tallest buildings in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_tallest_buildings_in_Hong_Kong) and are among the tallest in the Asia-Pacific region.[^cite_note-200] Other distinctive buildings lining the Hong Kong Island skyline include the [HSBC Main Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC_Building_\(Hong_Kong\)), the [anemometer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anemometer)-topped triangular [Central Plaza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Plaza_\(Hong_Kong\)), the circular [Hopewell Centre](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hopewell_Centre_\(Hong_Kong\)), and the sharp-edged [Bank of China Tower](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bank_of_China_Tower_\(Hong_Kong\)).[^cite_note-FengShui-201][^cite_note-202]



[HSBC Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HSBC_Building_\(Hong_Kong\))&#x20;

Demand for new construction has contributed to the frequent demolition of older buildings, freeing space for modern high-rises.[^cite_note-203] However, many examples of European and [Lingnan architecture](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lingnan_architecture) are still found throughout the territory. Older government buildings are examples of colonial architecture. The 1846 [Flagstaff House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Flagstaff_House,_Hong_Kong), the former residence of the [Commander of the British forces in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_Forces_Overseas_Hong_Kong), is the oldest Western-style building in Hong Kong.[^cite_note-204] Some buildings, such as the [Court of Final Appeal Building](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Court_of_Final_Appeal_Building) and the [Hong Kong Observatory](21292238.md) retain their original functions, and others have been [adapted and reused](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adaptive_reuse); the [Former Marine Police Headquarters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Former_Marine_Police_Headquarters) was redeveloped into a commercial and retail complex,[^cite_note-TourismFacts-205] and [Béthanie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/B%C3%A9thanie_\(Hong_Kong\)) (built in 1875 as a [sanatorium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sanatorium)) houses the [Hong Kong Academy for Performing Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Academy_for_Performing_Arts).[^cite_note-206] The [Tin Hau Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tin_Hau_Temple,_Joss_House_Bay), dedicated to the sea goddess [Mazu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazu) (originally built in 1012 and rebuilt in 1266), is the territory's oldest existing structure.[^cite_note-207] The [Ping Shan Heritage Trail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ping_Shan_Heritage_Trail) has architectural examples from several imperial Chinese dynasties, including the [Tsui Sing Lau Pagoda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tsui_Sing_Lau_Pagoda) (Hong Kong's only remaining pagoda).[^cite_note-208]

*[Tong lau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tong_lau)*, mixed-use tenement buildings constructed during the colonial era, blended southern Chinese architectural styles with European influences. These were especially prolific during the immediate post-war period, when many were rapidly built to house large numbers of Chinese migrants.[^cite_note-209] Examples include [Lui Seng Chun](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lui_Seng_Chun), the [Blue House](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Blue_House_\(Hong_Kong\)) in [Wan Chai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wan_Chai), and the [Shanghai Street shophouses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nos._600%E2%80%93626_Shanghai_Street) in [Mong Kok](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mong_Kok). Mass-produced [public-housing estates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_housing_in_Hong_Kong), built since the 1960s, are mainly constructed in [modernist style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_architecture).[^cite_note-210]

Skyline at night, with building lights reflected in water

The Hong Kong Island skyline, viewed from the [Victoria Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Harbour) waterfront



City view of Kowloon, Hong Kong Island, and the Hong Kong skyline

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Hong_Kong) and [Hong Kong people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_people)

| Cause of Death | 2001 | 2011 | 2021 |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1. Malignant neoplasms | 169.9 | 187.2 | 203.8 |
| 2. Pneumonia | 45.1 | 87.8 | **132.6** |
| 3. Diseases of heart | 70 | 89.6 | 89 |
| 4. Cerebrovascular | 46.6 | 47.2 | 42.2 |
| 5. External causes of morbidity and mortality | 27.5 | 22.2 | 26.7 |
| 6. Nephritis, nephrotic syndrome and nephrosis | 15.7 | 21.8 | 24 |
| 7. **Dementia** | 3.8 | 10.6 | **20.2** |
| 8. Septicaemia | 6.3 | 10.8 | 16.8 |
| 9. Chronic lower respiratory diseases | 31.5 | 27.8 | 14.3 |
| 10. Diabetes mellitus | 10.1 | 6.5 | 7.4 |
| All other causes | 69.7 | 85 | 118.1 |
| All causes | 496 | 596.6 | 695.2 |

Population graph

2016 [population pyramid](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_pyramid)

The [Census and Statistics Department](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Census_and_Statistics_Department_\(Hong_Kong\)) estimated Hong Kong's population at 7,413,070 in 2021. The overwhelming majority (91.6%) is [Chinese](372983.md),[^cite_note-demographics-8] most of whom are [Taishanese](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sze_Yap_people_in_Hong_Kong), [Teochew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Teochew_people), [Hakka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hakka_people), and other [Cantonese peoples](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cantonese_people).[^cite_note-212][^cite_note-213][^cite_note-214] The remaining 8.4% are non-ethnic Chinese minorities, primarily [Filipinos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Filipinos_in_Hong_Kong), [Indonesians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Indonesians_in_Hong_Kong), and [South Asians](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/South_Asians_in_Hong_Kong).[^cite_note-demographics-8][^cite_note-215] However, most Filipinos and Indonesians in Hong Kong are short-term workers. According to a 2021 thematic report by the Hong Kong government, after excluding foreign domestic helpers, the real number of non-Chinese ethnic minorities in the city was 301,344, or 4% of Hong Kong's population.[^cite_note-216] About half the population have [some form of British nationality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_nationality_law), a legacy of colonial rule; 3.4 million residents have [British National (Overseas)](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/British_National_\(Overseas\)) status, and 260,000 British citizens live in the territory.[^cite_note-217] The vast majority also hold [Chinese nationality](4600725.md), automatically granted to all ethnic Chinese residents at the handover.[^cite_note-218] Headline [population density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Population_density) exceeds 7,060 people/km<sup>2</sup> It is the [fourth-highest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density) in the world.[^cite_note-219]



[Wong Tai Sin Temple](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wong_Tai_Sin_Temple_\(Hong_Kong\)) is dedicated to the [Taoist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism) deity [Wong Tai Sin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wong_Tai_Sin).

Among the religious population, the traditional "[three teachings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_teachings)" of China, [Buddhism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chinese_Buddhism), [Confucianism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Confucianism), and [Taoism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taoism), have the most adherents (20%), followed by [Christianity](5211.md) (12%) and [Islam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Hong_Kong) (4%).[^cite_note-religionFacts-220] Followers of other religions, including [Sikhism](27964.md), [Hinduism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hinduism), and [Judaism](15624.md), generally originate from regions where their religion predominates.[^cite_note-religionFacts-220]

Life expectancy in Hong Kong was 82.8 years for males and 88.4 years for females at birth in 2024.[^cite_note-221] The birth rate in 2023 was 0.751 per woman of child-bearing age.[^cite_note-222] [Cancer](105219.md), [pneumonia](52135.md), [heart disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cardiovascular_disease), [cerebrovascular disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cerebrovascular_disease), and accidents are the territory's five leading causes of death.[^cite_note-2017HealthFacts-223] The [universal public healthcare system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care) is funded by general-tax revenue, and treatment is highly subsidised; on average, 95% of healthcare costs are covered by the government.[^cite_note-224]

The city has a severe amount of income inequality,[^cite_note-225] which has risen since the handover, as the region's ageing population has gradually added to the number of nonworking people.[^cite_note-226] Although median household income steadily increased during the decade to 2021, the wage gap remained high;[^cite_note-IncomeDistribution-227] the 90th percentile of earners receive 41% of all income.[^cite_note-IncomeDistribution-227] The city has the most billionaires per capita, with one billionaire per 109,657 people,[^cite_note-228] as well as the [second-highest number of billionaires](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_by_number_of_billionaires) of any city in the world,[^cite_note-229] the highest number of billionaires of any city in Asia, and the second largest concentration of [ultra high-net-worth individuals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ultra_high-net-worth_individual) of any city in the world.[^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231] Despite government efforts to reduce the growing disparity,[^cite_note-232] median income for the top 10% of earners is 57 times that of the bottom 10%.[^cite_note-233][^cite_note-234]

## Economy

Main articles: [Economy of Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Hong_Kong) and [Tourism in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Hong_Kong)



[Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Hong_Kong) is one of the world's [busiest container ports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_busiest_container_ports).

Along with [Singapore](27318.md), [South Korea](27019.md), and [Taiwan](25734.md), Hong Kong is one of the [Four Asian Tigers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Four_Asian_Tigers).

One of the world's most significant financial centres and commercial ports,[^cite_note-235] Hong Kong has a [market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Market_economy) focused on [services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Service_economy), characterised by [low taxation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxation_in_Hong_Kong), minimal government market intervention, and an established international financial market.[^cite_note-236] It is the world's [39th-largest economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)), with a [nominal GDP](12594.md) of approximately US\$446 billion.[^cite_note-IMFWEO.HK-15] Hong Kong is highly developed, and ranks fourth on the [UN Human Development Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index).[^cite_note-cia-169] The [Hong Kong Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Stock_Exchange) is the [fifth-largest in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_stock_exchanges), with a market capitalisation of HK\$48.2 trillion (US\$6.17 trillion) as of December 2025.[^cite_note-237] Hong Kong was ranked as the 15th most innovative territory in the 2025 [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index),[^cite_note-238][^cite_note-239] and 3rd in the [Global Financial Centres Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Financial_Centres_Index).[^cite_note-GFCI-240] The city is sometimes referred to as "Silicon Harbour" in the 1990s, a nickname derived from [Silicon Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silicon_Valley) in [California](5407.md).[^cite_note-Tech_Monitor_1999-241]

Hong Kong is the ninth largest trading entity in [exports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_exports) and eighth largest in [imports](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_imports) (2021),[^cite_note-CIAExports-242][^cite_note-CIAImports-243] trading more goods in value than its gross domestic product.[^cite_note-CIAExports-242][^cite_note-CIAImports-243] Over half of its cargo throughput consists of [transshipments](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transshipment) (goods travelling through Hong Kong). Products from mainland China account for about 40% of that traffic.[^cite_note-LegcoEconomy-244] The city's location allowed it to establish a transportation and logistics infrastructure, which includes the world's seventh-busiest container port[^cite_note-245] and the busiest airport for international cargo.[^cite_note-246] The territory's largest export markets are mainland China and the United States.[^cite_note-cia-169] Hong Kong is a key part of the [21st Century Maritime Silk Road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/21st_Century_Maritime_Silk_Road).[^cite_note-247] It has little arable land and few natural resources, importing most of its food and raw materials. More than 90% of Hong Kong's food is imported, including nearly all of its meat and rice.[^cite_note-248] Agricultural activity is 0.1% of GDP and consists of growing premium food and flower varieties.[^cite_note-249]

Although the territory had one of Asia's largest manufacturing economies during the latter half of the colonial era, Hong Kong's economy is now dominated by the service sector. The sector generates 92.7% of economic output, with the public sector accounting for about 10%.[^cite_note-250] Between 1961 and 1997, Hong Kong's gross domestic product increased by a factor of 180, and per capita GDP increased by a factor of 87.[^cite_note-251][^cite_note-252] The territory's GDP relative to mainland China's peaked at 27% in 1993; it fell to less than 3% in 2017, as the mainland developed and liberalised its economy.[^cite_note-253] Economic and infrastructure integration with China has increased significantly since the 1978 start of the [reform and opening up](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reform_and_opening_up) on the mainland. Since resumption of [cross-boundary train service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Guangzhou%E2%80%93Kowloon_through_train) in 1979, many rail and road links have been improved and constructed, facilitating trade between regions.[^cite_note-254][^cite_note-HZMBridge-255] The [Closer Economic Partnership Arrangement](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mainland_and_Hong_Kong_Closer_Economic_Partnership_Arrangement) formalised a policy of free trade between the two areas, with each jurisdiction pledging to remove remaining obstacles to trade and cross-boundary investment.[^cite_note-MainlandCEPA-256] A similar economic partnership with Macau details the liberalisation of trade between the special administrative regions.[^cite_note-257] Chinese companies have expanded their economic presence in the territory since the handover. Mainland firms represent over half of the [Hang Seng Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hang_Seng_Index) value, up from 5% in 1997.[^cite_note-258][^cite_note-259]

Large, empty room, with many desks and computer terminals

Former trading floor of the [Hong Kong Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Stock_Exchange)

As the mainland economy liberalised, Hong Kong's shipping industry faced intense competition from other Chinese ports. Half of China's trade goods were routed through Hong Kong in 1997, dropping to about 13% by 2015.[^cite_note-Commons5R18-260] The territory's minimal taxation, common law system, and civil service attract overseas corporations wishing to establish a presence in Asia.[^cite_note-Commons5R18-260] The city has the second-highest number of corporate headquarters in the Asia-Pacific region.[^cite_note-261] Hong Kong is a gateway for [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment) in China, giving investors open access to mainland Chinese markets through direct links with [the Shanghai](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shanghai-Hong_Kong_Stock_Connect) and [Shenzhen stock exchanges](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shenzhen-Hong_Kong_Stock_Connect). The territory was the first market outside mainland China for [renminbi-denominated bonds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dim_sum_bond), and is one of the largest hubs for offshore [renminbi](66958.md) trading.[^cite_note-262] In November 2020, Hong Kong's Financial Services and the Treasury Bureau proposed a new law that will restrict cryptocurrency trading to professional investors only, leaving amateur traders (93% of Hong Kong's trading population) out of the market.[^cite_note-263] The [Hong Kong dollar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_dollar), the local currency, is the eighth-most traded currency in the world.[^cite_note-264] Due to extremely compact house sizes and the extremely high [housing density](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Urban_density), the city has the [most expensive housing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_houses_in_Hong_Kong) market in the world.[^cite_note-265][^cite_note-266][^cite_note-267]

The government has had a passive role in the economy. Colonial governments had little [industrial policy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Industrial_policy) and implemented almost no [trade controls](4510742.md). Under the doctrine of "[positive non-interventionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Positive_non-interventionism)", post-war administrations deliberately avoided direct resource allocation; active intervention was considered detrimental to economic growth.[^cite_note-ceo-268] While the economy transitioned to a service basis during the 1980s,[^cite_note-ceo-268] late colonial governments introduced interventionist policies. Post-handover administrations continued and expanded these programmes, including [export-credit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Export_credit_agency) guarantees, a [compulsory pension scheme](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mandatory_Provident_Fund), a [minimum wage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimum_Wage_Ordinance), [anti-discrimination laws](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Anti-discrimination_law), and a state mortgage backer.[^cite_note-EconomistEnd-269]

In April 2026, Hong Kong's Securities and Futures Commission launched a regulatory framework to enable secondary trading of tokenised investment products on licensed virtual asset platforms, aiming to broaden retail investors' access to regulated digital asset trading services.[^cite_note-270]

### Tourism

&#x20;Tourism is a major part of Hong Kong's economy, accounting for 5% of its GDP.[^cite_note-TourismFacts-205] In 2016, 26.6 million visitors contributed HK\$258 billion (US\$32.9 billion) to the territory, making Hong Kong the [14th-most popular destination for international tourists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Tourism_rankings). It is the most popular Chinese city for tourists, receiving over 70% more visitors than its closest competitor [Macau](19068.md).[^cite_note-271] The city is ranked as one of the [most expensive cities for expatriates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_most_expensive_cities_for_expatriate_employees).[^cite_note-272][^cite_note-273] However, since 2020, there has been a sharp decline in incoming visitors due to tight COVID-19 travel restrictions. In an attempt to attract tourists back to Hong Kong, the Hong Kong government announced plans to give away 500,000 free airline tickets in 2023.[^cite_note-274] Hong Kong was the second most visited city in the world with 23.2 million visitors in 2025.[^cite_note-275]



[Victoria Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Peak) is a major tourist attraction that offers views of [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central,_Hong_Kong) and [Victoria Harbour](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Harbour).

## Infrastructure

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Hong Kong](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Hong_Kong)

Tunnel entrance at night, with heavy traffic

[Hong Kong taxis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxis_of_Hong_Kong)

Hong Kong has a highly developed and sophisticated transport network. Over 90% of daily trips made by its residents are made with public transport, the highest percentage in the world.[^cite_note-pubtransport-276] The [Octopus card](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Octopus_card), a [contactless](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Contactless_payment) [smart](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Smart_card) [payment](1226927.md) card made for Hong Kong, is widely accepted on railways, trams, buses and ferries, and can also be used for payment in most retail stores.[^cite_note-277] Alternative payments such as [Apple Pay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Apple_Pay), [AliPay](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alipay), [Mastercard](378791.md), and [Visa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visa_Inc.) have also been gradually introduced as methods of payment for public transport.[^cite_note-278][^cite_note-\:5-279][^cite_note-280][^cite_note-281][^cite_note-282]



Hong Kong buses and trams

Hong Kong's EV policy, driven by the "Hong Kong Roadmap on Popularisation of Electric Vehicles," aims to achieve zero vehicular emissions before 2050. Key strategies include banning new registration of fuel-propelled private cars, including hybrids, by 2035 or earlier, expanding charging infrastructure, and offering significant, extended tax concessions such as the One-for-One Replacement Scheme (expiring 31 March 2026).[^cite_note-283]

#### Land

The [Peak Tram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_Tram), Hong Kong's first public transport system, has provided [funicular](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Funicular) rail transport between [Central](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central,_Hong_Kong) and [Victoria Peak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Victoria_Peak) since 1888.[^cite_note-284]

The [Central and Western District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_and_Western_District) has an extensive system of escalators and [moving pavements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Moving_walkway), which, together with the [Mid-Levels escalator system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central%E2%80%93Mid-Levels_escalator), is the world's longest outdoor covered escalator system.[^cite_note-285]

[Hong Kong Tramways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Tramways)' tram network covers a portion of Hong Kong Island, covering from [Kennedy Town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kennedy_Town) to [Shau Kei Wan](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Shau_Kei_Wan), with a branch to [Happy Valley](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Happy_Valley,_Hong_Kong). It operates 6 routes and has had a ridership of 42,558 in 2022.[^cite_note-286][^cite_note-\:5-279] It began servicing Hong Kong since 1904.[^cite_note-287] Hong Kong Tramways currently holds the Guinness World Record as the "Largest double-decker tram fleet in service", certified on 30 July 2021, with a fleet of 165 double-decker trams.[^cite_note-288] The fleet of trams was mostly built by Hong Kong Tramways.

&#x20;The [Mass Transit Railway](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MTR) (MTR) is an extensive passenger rail network, connecting 99 [metro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rapid_transit) stations and 68 [light-rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Light_Rail_\(MTR\)) stops throughout the territory.[^cite_note-RailwayFacts-289][^cite_note-290] With a daily ridership of almost five million, the system serves 41% of all public transit passengers in the city[^cite_note-LegcoTransport-291] and has an on-time rate of 99.9%.[^cite_note-292]



[Hong Kong Peak Tram](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Peak_Tram)

&#x20;The [East Rail line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East_Rail_line) offers metro services to [Shenzhen](83368.md). Its southern terminus is [Admiralty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Admiralty_station_\(MTR\)). [Connecting service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_Express_Rail_Link) to the [national high-speed rail system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/China_Railway_High-speed) is provided at [West Kowloon railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hong_Kong_West_Kowloon_railway_station).[^cite_note-293]

Light-rail train on a straightaway

MTR train on the [Tung Chung line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tung_Chung_line)

Although public transport systems handle most passenger traffic, over 500,000 private vehicles are registered in Hong Kong.[^cite_note-294] Unlike in mainland China, automobiles in Hong Kong drive [on the left](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Left-_and_right-hand_traffic) due to the historical influence of the British Empire.[^cite_note-295] Vehicle traffic is extremely congested in urban areas, exacerbated by limited space to expand roads and an increasing number of vehicles.[^cite_note-296] More than 18,000 [taxicabs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Taxis_of_Hong_Kong), easily identifiable by their bright colours and taxi lights, are licensed to carry riders in the territory.[^cite_note-297] Unlicensed ride-hailing services such as [Uber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Uber) exist in Hong Kong as well, with some operating with licensed taxis to legitimise their business in ride-sharing.
